## Working doctrine (owner ruling, 2026-09-19)

**This backlog is the single source of truth for work.** From 2026-09-19 onwards, nothing is designed, implemented, or merged unless it is **first referenced from — or added to — this table**. New work: add a row here (id, severity, description) *before* planning it; then design → implement → merge against that row. This keeps every change traceable to an agreed item and stops scope entering through the side door.

Severity: **P1** = `main` is broken and every merge is blocked · **P2** = active in-scope milestone work · **P3** = real but low-consequence hardening · **P4** = minor/cosmetic · **P5** = trivial · **OWNER** = needs an owner decision. Status: **Open** = filed, not yet built · **Done** = landed · **Deferred** = read once, not scheduled · **Waived** = consciously not doing (reason given) · **Owner decision** = awaiting a ruling.

## In-scope milestone — the dynamic user-driven pipeline (M9)

The active build rows (owner go-ahead 2026-09-21; one fix at a time, an approval per fix, CI only at the merge). Design, dependencies and per-row detail live in the plan `quirky-enchanting-flurry.md`. Listed in **landing order**: T440, the pure registry refactor, lands first and alone so every later behaviour change is attributable to the row that made it. T442 has no dependency on T440 and can be built in parallel.

| Item | Severity | Status | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| T440 | P2 | Done 2026-09-21 | Single pipeline registry: collapse the 6+ duplicated phase/gate facts into one resolver in `stage.py`. Behaviour-preserving refactor. Landed via PR #14 (`stage.pipeline`, `stage.Gate`/`GATES`, `state.PHASES` explicit; derived `approve.GATE_PHASE`, `validate.MERGE_GATE`, `slice.LAST_GATE`, `deploy.GATE`, the per-module `PHASE` constants; a genericity guard). |
| T441 | P2 | Done 2026-09-23 (a #15, b+c #16) | User-driven pipeline: locked steps, `extra_gates`, gate re-anchoring, `required_phases`/`required_gates` floors, CI merge check covering added gates. **Lands in three PRs (the 2-PR split grew a third once the gate machinery was measured):** **T441a — locked steps (DONE, PR #15):** `stage.LOCKED_PHASES = (intake, implementation, review)`, refused by name in `declared()`; `closeout` deliberately unlocked (slice supports a `phases[]` stopping at `triage`). **T441b — extra_gates (DONE):** `extra_gates: [step]` → a hard `G-<step>` gate in `stage.pipeline`, halting the run (`halted_at` iterates the pipeline's gates) and recordable by `approve` (`gate_phase_of` + `record`/CLI widened past the `G1–G5` assumption; `artifacts_through`/`after` made phase-based so an extra gate's approval pins the right artifacts). No merge-guarantee touched. **T441c — enforcement + governance (DONE, PR #16):** the orphan reconciliation in `stage.pipeline` (a builtin gate whose step the user deleted is RE-ANCHORED to the nearest preceding active step — keeping G4 alive when `triage` is deleted — while a gate whose phase needs an ABSENT CAPABILITY is dropped into a new `Pipeline.dropped_gates` diagnostic channel; two builtins re-anchored onto one step merge, merge gate kept); `stage.resolved_hard_gate_map`/`resolved_hard_gates` as the reconciled hard-gate accessor and `policy.hard_gates(..., standing=)` to filter orphans (the `policy`→`stage` cycle keeps the reconciliation on the stage side); `approve.gate_phase_of` now follows a re-anchored gate; `approve.record` stamps `resolved_gates` ({gate: phase}) into every entry and `validate.merge_ready` demands every reached hard gate over unmoved bytes (reading the stamp, never importing `stage`), with the CLI scope line widened; the `required_phases`/`required_gates` tighten-only floors (new `policy.DEFAULTS` keys, `validate` clauses, `tighten_only(..., phases=, gates=)` enforced at `approve.record`); `extra_gates`/`required_phases`/`required_gates` added to the committed schemas. **Measured during T440:** a hard gate whose phase is inactive is a silent orphan — `policy.hard_gates` still calls it hard while the new `stage.pipeline` drops it — and this ships LIVE in the default install (G1 stands at `scoping`/P2, which the default omits and which needs the absent graph capability, so G1 is unenforced out of the box). CI's `validate.merge_ready` also enforces only G4, never the resolved `hard_gates`. T441 must reconcile the orphan (re-anchor / refuse / diagnose — the capability-skip case as well as user deletion) and make `merge_ready` require every resolved hard gate the run reached. |
| T450 | P2 | Done 2026-09-23 (a #17, b #18, c #19) | Pipeline visualisation, generated from `resolve()`: an ASCII diagram in `stage --list`, `--diagram mermaid` written to a committed, deterministic `.sdlc/pipeline.md` with a drift guard, the run's position rendered by `status`, and a wizard preview. **Lands in three PRs:** **T450a** — one renderer in `stage.py` off `stage.pipeline()`: an ASCII diagram above `--list`'s rows (gates heaviest, skipped steps and dropped/re-anchored gates named with their reason), `--diagram mermaid` writing a deterministic `.sdlc/pipeline.md` (flowchart + step/gate table, no run id or timestamp), and a `--check` drift guard. **T450b** — `status` renders the run's position on the same diagram, AND fixes a defect measured 2026-09-23 while planning: `status.PHASE_GATE` (`status.py:30`) still derives from the static builtin `approve.GATE_PHASE`, so a run halted at an `extra_gates` stop (`G-test`) or at a RE-ANCHORED builtin (G1@P1 in the default install) is reported as waiting on "a gate" rather than by name — `status` must read the resolved pipeline. **T450c** — the installer writes `.sdlc/pipeline.md` at install (it already spawns a harness tool the same way for `validate --emit-check`). **T450a DONE 2026-09-23 (PR #17):** `stage.render_text`/`render_mermaid`, the ASCII `--list` diagram (box-drawing dropped after measuring that `═` carries cp1252-undefined `0x90`, blanking a default-decoding Windows caller's output), `--diagram mermaid` + `--check`, `--list` rows naming the gates that stand. **T450b DONE 2026-09-23 (PR #18):** `status.standing_gates` reads the resolved pipeline (the static `PHASE_GATE` is gone), naming `G-test` and a re-anchored builtin; an unresolvable profile is said once, never silent; `status --run` draws the run on `stage.render_text` with done/here/terminal step marks, live gate verdicts or `waiting`, and loop-backs counted from history. **T450c DONE 2026-09-23 (PR #19):** the installer draws `.sdlc/pipeline.md` LAST — after every write and the graph build, since both change the pipeline — by spawning `stage.py --diagram mermaid`, reported in the file list's vocabulary (create / unchanged / update), `- skipped` with its reason for a standalone installer, `x BLOCKED` with the renderer's words on failure (never fatal), and a `[dry-run]` line. **Deferred to T371/T448:** the wizard preview, which needs `sdlc config`; T450a only keeps the renderer a callable the wizard can reuse. `--diagram d2` stays out. |
| T442 | P2 | Done 2026-09-23 (#20) | Pre-INIT prerequisites: a `[project]` metadata template (name, description, owner, criticality mandatory), validated and rendered into `profile.yml`; stop-and-fill enforcement. **Owner ruling 2026-09-23: the four mandatory fields are `name`, `description`, `owner`, `criticality` (low\|medium\|high); nothing else is mandatory** - `default_branch` is detectable (pre-filled from git), `compliance`/`issue_tracker` are often honestly empty, and a required field invites a junk value. **Built 2026-09-23:** `sdlc-project.toml` (single repo) or a workspace `[project]` table, read by `read_project` with the workspace `_ws_*` validators; `project_gate` runs FIRST in `install()` - missing file -> template written (name + default_branch pre-filled), blanks named, exit 2, nothing else written; `--dry-run` writes nothing; a workspace without a `[project]` table uses the target's `sdlc-project.toml` too - the installer never edits the developer's own workspace TOML (an early cut that appended the table wrote 34 lines into a committed test fixture); rendered as a `project:` block in profile.yml, read at run time by `policy.profile_project` (by block, never `profile_find`'s any-depth match); P0 states it as the `project` value (a pre-T442 install gets a stated absence); `criticality: high` suggests `gate_profile: team`, never forces it. |
| T445 | P2 | Done 2026-09-23 (a #21, b #24, c #26) | Per-step provider and model: a `models:` block in `profile.yml` (default `openai/gpt-4o-mini`, dormant behind `model_routing: off`), a `providers.toml` catalog, an `anthropic-messages` adapter plus `--provider` in `http_runner.py`, `{provider}`/`{model}` placeholders in runner commands, key-presence refusal at step entry, a cost acknowledgement, and the resolved model shown and recorded. **Lands in three PRs:** **T445a** — configuration and resolution, INERT by default: `model_routing: off` + `models: {default: openai/gpt-4o-mini}` in the profile template, `sdlc_templates/providers.toml` (protocol, base_url, key_var per provider; optional prices), `policy.model_routing`/`models_declared`/`model_for(target, step)` refusing by name (unknown step, malformed `provider/model`, unknown provider, a missing key only when routing is ON), the resolution order (`phase_runners:` wins -> `models:<step>` -> `models:default`), `stage --list` showing each step's resolved actor (driving tool / runner / provider/model), the schema, a guard that no `tools/sdlc/*.py` names a provider or model, and an inertness test (routing off: no key read, no network). **T445b** — `http_runner.py`: an `anthropic-messages` adapter and `--provider` resolving protocol/base_url/key_var from the catalog; spawned against a local stub HTTP server only (no paid call). **T445b built 2026-09-23:** `--protocol anthropic-messages` (x-api-key + pinned `anthropic-version`, `system` beside the messages, `max_tokens` always sent, text blocks joined, an empty answer refused at exit 2) and `--provider NAME [--catalog PATH]` reading T445a's `providers.toml` with stdlib `tomllib` (OUTSIDE_PROCESS: no harness import), explicit flags winning, the provider's own `key_var` named when missing, and `OPENAI_*` env conveniences confined to item 104's no-provider path. **T445c built 2026-09-23:** `drive.route_for` (one answer for step, `--plan`, `--spend`): a model-endpoint step becomes the endpoint runner NAMED `provider/model` (so `--spend` acknowledges the model and a changed model is refused like a changed runner); | T371a | LAST | Done 2026-09-23 | Wizard ENGINE, non-interactive and scriptable (an agent's tool call has no TTY): `sdlc config --spec` prints the question set as JSON DERIVED from the committed schemas (id, prompt, type, choices, default, the key it writes), so questions cannot drift from what is valid; `sdlc config set <key>=<value>` and `sdlc config apply --from answers.json` validate and write `.sdlc/profile.yml`, or `sdlc-project.toml` before INIT; every accepted change prints the resolved pipeline as it will be and rewrites `.sdlc/pipeline.md`; `sdlc config --check` re-validates. Refusals by name, never a partial write. Spawned CLI tests. **Design, fixed before code (2026-09-23):** `tools/sdlc/config.py`, reached as `sdlc config` through T448's derived dispatcher. (1) The questions: project metadata (types and the criticality enum from the committed `workspace.schema.json` `[project]` table, required-ness from the installer's `PROJECT_REQUIRED`), then `phases`, `gate_profile`, `extra_gates`, `gates.<gate>`, `signing`, `model_routing`, `models.default` and `models.<step>` (choices and patterns from the committed `profile.schema.json`), each with its current value read through the harness's own readers. (2) Validation is what a RUN would refuse, not a second rulebook: the profile reader's refusals, `stage.pipeline`, `tighten_only` with the resolved phases and hard gates (as `approve.record` calls it), and `models.model_for` for every active step. It runs on the CANDIDATE before a byte is written, through a what-if seam in `policy._profile_source` (the one door every profile reader goes through), and every written key is read back through those readers before the write. (3) Writes are line edits that keep every comment, a whole change or none of it, line endings kept; `project.*` writes `sdlc-project.toml` (from the template before INIT) and, once installed, the profile's `project:` block too, since INIT never rewrites an edited profile; `.sdlc/pipeline.md` is redrawn after every change. Before INIT the pipeline keys are refused by name. Exit 0 applied, 1 refused (nothing written), 2 could not look or bad input. `--dry-run` previews; `--json` for a front end. **Built 2026-09-23:** `tools/sdlc/config.py` as designed, plus `review.block_on` as a question - found by the first test run: `regulated` also refuses a `block_on` without `medium`, so a spec offering the choice without that question offered one the wizard could never apply. `policy.proposed_profile` is the what-if seam (a context manager; `_profile_source` answers the proposed text for that resolved target only, and restores it on the way out). New keys land under the template's commented example (`# extra_gates:`, `# gates:`); an emptied block loses its header. The read-back caught a real reader limit in its own test: `profile_project` splits `compliance` on commas, so a tag holding one is refused, not stored as two. `tools/tests/test_config_wizard.py` (43 tests, spawned; one real install per module) - three mutants killed (no read-back; validating the file on disk instead of the candidate; a stale `project:` block). The lead's review before merge found three more, each fixed red-first: `set` skipped a project value the file already held, so the repair `--check` names did nothing; a workspace-carried `project:` block (no `sdlc-project.toml`) would have been overwritten from the bare template, now refused by name; lists are written in the lifecycle's order. README gains `Or answer questions: sdlc config`. |
| T371b | LAST | Done 2026-09-24 | Wizard FRONT ENDS over T371a: a `/sdlc-config` entry point fanned out by the installer (the agent reads `--spec`, asks one question at a time in chat, applies each with `config set`), and `sdlc config --interactive`, a stdlib `input()` loop that REFUSES when stdin is not a TTY and names the other mode instead of hanging. The pre-INIT stop-and-fill message names `sdlc config`. Depends on T371a (merged, #37). **Design, fixed before code (2026-09-24; owner go "go on T371b"):** (1) `sdlc_templates/CONFIG-SKILL.md`, fanned out exactly as the graph skill is: its own `sdlc-config` file for the per-file tools (Claude Code's `.claude/skills/sdlc-config/SKILL.md` is the `/sdlc-config` command; Cursor and Windsurf rules), and a section of the ONE harness block for the block tools (Copilot, Gemini, Aider, Codex's AGENTS.md). It teaches the chat loop - read `--spec`, ask one question at a time, `--dry-run` before a pipeline change, one `set` for answers that only make sense together, explain a refusal, finish with `--check` - and never hand-editing a key `--spec` lists. (3) INIT's stop-and-fill message for `sdlc-project.toml` names the `sdlc config` commands (`--interactive`, `set project.<field>=...`) when the harness is beside the installer. **Built 2026-09-24:** as designed. `config_skill` / `config_tool_files` in `sdlc-init.py` fan `sdlc_templates/CONFIG-SKILL.md` out beside the graph skill (the block tools' section comes after the lifecycle's and the graph's, one block per file kept); a lone bundle ships none (tested). `sdlc config --interactive` asks in `input()`, previews through `plan`, writes only on a yes; a refusal offers another pass with every answer kept, so `regulated` can be finished rather than retyped; input ending mid-way writes nothing (exit 2). **Measured on Windows:** `isatty()` is True for the NUL device (`stdin=DEVNULL`), which answers every read with EOF - the loop STARTED there and hit end-of-input instead of refusing by name - so `config.is_terminal` asks `GetConsoleMode` (regression test; the plain-`isatty` mutant is killed by three tests; CLAUDE.md's Windows note records it). Only a start whose actor is `driving-tool` is closed: the first cut closed `drive`'s own start, because `drive` advances BEFORE it writes its finish - measured, a double count - and a `--report-usage` finish already closes the step. `drive`'s attempt counter skips driving-tool starts; `step_started` gains `actor`. `tools/tests/test_driving_tool_timing.py`. |

The broken-chain refusal T446a added has no test anywhere, which is how its placement went unnoticed. Not affected: `drive` appends `step_started` BEFORE it spawns a runner, and `stage.py --report-usage` only appends. **Fix - check first, then write; no rollback:** an `events.require_appendable` preflight (the predicate `append` already uses) called before the first durable write in `state.advance` and `approve.record` (beside approve's existing check of its own ledger's last line), and `state.create` resolving its imports and `policy.profile_project` before it makes the folder. The residual window - an I/O failure between a write and its append - is stated, not rolled back: undoing an append-only file is itself an edit CI refuses. **Guard (T463's class):** every `tools/sdlc/*.py` with a `__main__` block that imports `tools.` must find the harness when run as a script - spawned under `python -S` from an empty cwd, checked by where `tools.sdlc` resolves AFTER the script's own setup, not by `--help`'s exit (pre-T463 `state.py --help` exits 0, which is why T448's per-subcommand spawns stayed green); measured 2026-09-23: 27 script modules, 24 import `tools.`, all pass, 3.2 s, none writes to its cwd; against the pre-T463 tree it flags exactly `state.py`. Tests spawned and red first. Plan: `t465-refuse-before-writing.md`. **Built 2026-09-23, landed via PR #36 (owner-approved; local CI passed: Linux suite 7149 passed, gitleaks clean):** `events.require_appendable` shares one predicate (`_break_at`) with `append`, and its refusal names the line and says nothing was written, which is true only because it runs first; `state.advance` asks it before EVERY save (owner default A); `approve.record` asks it beside its own ledger check, before `feedback.capture`; `state.create` imports and reads the profile before making the folder. `tools/tests/test_refuse_before_writing.py`: each refusal spawned under `-S` from outside the harness, with an intact-chain control beside it, and "wrote nothing" measured as every file under the target byte-identical - all 5 red against the unfixed source, each for the reason it exists (files left behind, both moves recorded, the approval writing `approvals.jsonl` + `feedback.jsonl`, the preflight missing). `tools/tests/test_script_modules_find_the_harness.py`: the guard runs over the `sdlc` dispatcher's own derived set - 27 tools, 24 needing the harness when built (28 and 25 on main once `config.py` joined in #37; the set is derived, so a tool added later is covered without an edit here); exempt are the two runners and the installer, and `policy.py` is IN only through its `importlib` string - under `python -E -S`; against the pre-T463 tree it fails exactly `[state]` (1 failed, 26 passed), plus a positive control on T463's synthetic shape. |

| T483 | P2 | Done 2026-09-25 | A Windows clone made with Git's default line-ending setting (`core.autocrlf=true`) fails every merge check: artifacts are pinned over raw bytes, and `sdlc-init.py` installs no `.gitattributes`, so `validate.py --ledger` exits 1 on an untouched clone. (T482 run 1: reproduced by the reviewer and re-run by the lead) Fix: install a `.gitattributes` that keeps `.sdlc/runs/` byte-exact. **Fixed 2026-09-25 (batch 2, line endings):** The installer writes `.sdlc/.gitattributes` (`runs/** -text`), its own file beside `.sdlc/.gitignore`: git gives the deeper file precedence for the paths it names, so the run folders check out byte for byte while the adopter's root `.gitattributes` is never edited and keeps applying everywhere else (measured against real git, with a root `eol=crlf` rule). A clone converted before the file existed still fails, since a pin is over exact bytes, but `state.drift` now says the file changed only in its line endings and prints the lossless `git checkout` that restores it. `test_ledger_gate.py`: a real `core.autocrlf=true` clone passes the `--ledger` check with the file, and an already-converted clone gets the new reason and is fixed by the command it prints; `test_init_chains_the_build.py`: the install writes the file and leaves the root one alone (all red before). **Review round (pinned Opus 5, 2026-09-25):** F1 (P2): the first line-endings remedy was right in one direction only. Pinned over CRLF and stored as LF on COMMIT, no checkout can restore the bytes, so telling that case to check out again sent a person round an endless loop. The two directions now get different remedies, `git checkout` and `git add --renormalize` on a copy that still has CRLF, and each is run in a test that then passes the check. F1b: the installer notes, when it first writes the file into a repository with committed runs, that the check will name any such file. F3 (P2): the same class stood in the graph snapshot, whose shards are digested byte for byte. They are now written LF (`emit.py`, a core change recorded in `core_baseline.md` by its own commit), and each shard directory carries its own `.gitattributes`, which `snapshot.write_shard` writes, because each component commits its shard in its own repository, which the installer never touches. It names only the three digested files, so the build's other outputs are not frozen at a platform newline. F5: a conversion is its own drift kind, `converted`, so `approve` still names the gate, `stage.stale` offers only the lossless remedy, and `feedback.capture` no longer re-pins a converted file as a person's edit - a path that predates this batch. F6: the probe is capped at 8 MiB. F8: the ledger tests spawn the CLI. `test_line_ending_drift.py` and new cases in `test_ledger_gate.py` and `test_snapshot_shards.py` (red before). **Review round 2:** F1-F8 confirmed closed, and the shard-attributes design endorsed. G1 (P2): `core_baseline.md` names the emit.py commit's own sha, which is not on main until this merges - so the branch is merged with a real merge commit, never rebased or squashed, and the sha's ancestry is checked on main after the merge (CLAUDE.md's rule that took main red once). G2: the checkout remedy was still a no-op when the converted bytes themselves were committed (an editor's CRLF under `-text`), and "a checkout converted them" was then untrue; the run's kept copy holds the pinned bytes whatever the commit holds, so it is the remedy whenever it is intact, in `record_command`'s placeholder form because the detail can reach a committed record, and a checkout only when the kept copy was converted too. G4: the feedback line keeps the remedy. G5: the upgrade note is silent in a dry run and no longer claims the runs were committed. Tests for each (red before). One test, the cloned shard loading clean, is red before only on Linux: on Windows the old CRLF shard was committed verbatim and git skips converting a blob that already holds CRLF. **Review round 3 (verdict MERGE):** G2, G4, G5, G6 confirmed, the restore remedy run end to end with real git. Two follow-ups fixed before merge: R1, an unreadable kept copy made the merge check a traceback (the G2 probe sat outside the read guard; it now falls back to the checkout remedy), and R2, the feedback line said line endings twice. Tests for both (red before). |

| T491 | P3 | Done 2026-09-27 | `stage.py --advance` accepts a move into a step the profile does not run and skips the checks when leaving it; the merge check still refuses on the stamped gate. (T482 run 1: reproduced by the reviewer and re-run by the lead) **Fixed in batch 10, measured wider than filed:** `advance` checked only the step it left, so a forward JUMP went ahead too - P0 -> P5 checked P0 alone, and the work and gates of P1, P3 and P4 were never asked for. Now a forward move enters the NEXT step the profile runs, never jumping one: refused naming the step to move to, which is how `drive` and `slice.walk` have always moved. The pinned review showed why asking about each jumped step instead does not work: a gate ahead of the run is one `approve` refuses to take, so the remedy could not be followed, and a step never entered never has its routing resolved or its time recorded (B10-3, B10-7). From a step the profile does not run, a move asks every running step behind the run. Entering a step the profile skips is refused by name, after the step left is asked, so the slice walk's move off the last step still proves that step's gates. `--force` records any of these overrides; a one-step move is asked exactly as before. |

Now `_shell_run_at` reads only the shell's own options (they end at its first operand) and returns a location; the ORIGINAL words after it are judged, and a clustered flag wins over a later bare `-c`. F6 (a T493 gap, not a regression): a value-taking option (`-o pipefail`, `--rcfile FILE`) or `+x` before the run flag is skipped, as xargs's value flags already are. Two more tests, each red before this round. **Review round 3 (verdict MERGE):** F9, a pre-existing gap of F6's class, fixed in the same batch - bash's `-O`/`+O` take a value word, so a run flag behind them was unread; added to the shell value-flag list. The reviewer validated the F5 fix over 6,160 command spellings with no regression, and F9's over 3,780. |
| T494 | P2 | Done 2026-09-26 | Quoted Windows paths in component, runner and `vcs:` commands are read with their YAML escapes left in, so the wrong program is spawned: `components()`, `runners_declared()` and `top_vcs()` use `_unquote` instead of `_scalar`, and the crosscheck cannot see sequence blocks. (T482 run 2: reproduced by the reviewer, not yet re-run by the lead) Fix: read those values through `_scalar`. **Fixed 2026-09-26 (batch 5, policy and config):** Reproduced by the lead against PyYAML. A component's `test_command`, a runner's `command` and the top-level `vcs.commit_command`, each written through the installer's `_yaml_dq`, kept their escaped quotes and doubled backslashes. They are read through `_text`: a quoted value is decoded as `_scalar` decodes it, and anything else is taken as written. `_scalar` also reads YAML's doubled single quote now. The crosscheck's own trigger, a third disagreement inside a sequence of maps, was met. So it now compares each `components:` and `runners:` entry's text values, a nested `vcs:` included, as the real entry readers read them (`_component_entries` and `_runner_entries`, split out for this) against PyYAML's, entry by entry. A known key that PyYAML reads and this reader dropped is a disagreement too. Tests red before: the installer-written command through all four readers, an entry disagreement refused by name, and the doubled quote. A control shows a nameless runner is no disagreement. **Review round (pinned Opus 5, 2026-09-26):** B5-2: `profile_project` and `models.models_declared` kept `_unquote`, and with `_scalar` taught the doubled quote, the crosscheck stopped refusing their misread. Both read through `_text` now, and `_unquote` is gone. B5-4: the entry crosscheck compares typed values through `_same_text`, and `deny_raw_git` reads every spelling PyYAML calls true. B5-5: a component's inline list (the installer's own `known_failures: [...]`) is read as a list, and lists are compared wherever the reader reads one. `_text` reads YAML's null as no text. The config wizard's read-back test had pinned the old comma split: it now proves the round trip, and the read-back guard is tested in-process with a reader made to misread. Tests red before. **Review round 2 (pinned Opus 5, 2026-09-26):** R2-3: the list comparison normalised only PyYAML's side and refused a `[true]` that both readers read alike. `_same_text` is on both sides now, at the top level too, where the same asymmetry predated batch 5. R2-2, B5-4's other half: the top-level comparison coerces the keys a reader types as booleans (`vcs.deny_raw_git`, `model_routing`) exactly as that reader does. So `deny_raw_git: yes` and `model_routing: on` now read alike, and `maybe` is a disagreement. `model_routing`'s spellings moved to `policy`, which the embedded ledger check may import and `models` may not. R2-6: reading YAML's null as no text made a component's `path: ~` the workspace root in silence, where it had failed by name before; it is now refused by entry. Tests red before. **Review round 3 (pinned Opus 5, 2026-09-26):** R3-1: the null-`path` refusal did not check depth, so it refused an adopter's nested `path: ~` under a component whose own path was plain. It now reads only the entry's own `path:`, at its keys' column, and says what to write (the path half of R3-3). R3-4, a behaviour change recorded here rather than left silent: `deny_raw_git: ""` and `model_routing: ""` now refuse, where round 1 read them as false. PyYAML keeps the empty string while the reader types a boolean, and nothing the harness writes spells them that way. Test red before. |

| T500 | P2 | Done 2026-09-25 | The test phase reports `verdict: green` at exit 0 when the test command exits non-zero, if the baseline was red, `known_failures` is declared and no JUnit report is written: `suite.judge` answers `unmeasurable`, which `_verdict`, `stopped` and `obligations` ignore. (T482 run 3: reproduced by the reviewer, not yet re-run by the lead) Fix: a non-zero exit that cannot be judged must hold the phase open. **Fixed 2026-09-25 (batch 4, phase verdicts):** Reproduced by the lead through the failing test. An `unmeasurable` build or test step whose command exited non-zero now holds its component (`suite._holds`), so the verdict is red and `stopped` names it. `obligations` holds P5 on everything `stopped` names rather than on `new-failures` alone, which also stops a red suite with no baseline passing P5. A step that passed and reported no count still does not hold. `test_suite.py`: two holds (red before) and the passing control. **Review round (pinned Opus 5, 2026-09-25):** B4-6: the scope-add proposal filtered its evidence on the old literal list, so a component held by a failing suite nobody could judge got a heading and nothing under it. It uses `_holds` now (red before). |
| T501 | P2 | Done 2026-09-25 | Security: `release.py --cut` puts the run title into a shell command unquoted (`TAG_DEFAULT`, run with `shell=True`). (T482 run 3: reproduced by the reviewer, not yet re-run by the lead) Fix: build the tag command as an argument list, without a shell. **Fixed 2026-09-25 (batch 1, security):** Reproduced by the lead through the failing test. The default tag runs as an argument list, without a shell. A declared command is filled only with values every shell reads as text; otherwise the step does not run and names the placeholder, and the artifact-version check follows the same rule. `test_release_cut.py`: the tag message equals a quoted title byte for byte, the declared refusal and the artifact check (red before), and a plain-title control. **Review round (pinned Opus 5, 2026-09-25):** F2, the first fix's ASCII allowlist refused an accented letter or an em dash for a reason that was not true. Now a value is refused only for a character bash or cmd reads as syntax (`_SHELL_SYNTAX`), and the refusal names it. The reviewer's narrower set (a double quote, `$`, a backtick, `%` and `!`) was not used: it assumes the value sits inside double quotes, and a template may place it bare, where `;`, `&` and a pipe are syntax too. `cut_problems` now carries a step's own reason instead of `exited None`. F3: the no-push test asserts on the tag command's text, since `in` on the new tuple asks for an equal element. **Review round 2:** F8, a stated limit rather than a change: a space in a bare placeholder word-splits (argument injection, not command injection); the value is the run's own and the shell-syntax set stops the break-out that matters. The reviewer confirmed the syntax set is the right choice over its own narrower one. The cost is named: an ordinary title with `#` or `%` now stops a declared step, with the character named; the default argv tag path is unaffected. |
| T502 | P2 | Done 2026-09-25 | A failed `git diff` (for example, a base commit lost to a rebase) is read as "nothing changed" (`suite.changed_files`, `check=False`), so the test phase runs nothing and reports green. (T482 run 3: reproduced by the reviewer, not yet re-run by the lead) Fix: surface git's error as a note, and never report green for zero executed steps. **Fixed 2026-09-25 (batch 4, phase verdicts):** Reproduced by the lead through the failing test. A failed `git diff` is a note carrying git's reason, and the component is run as changed rather than read as untouched. A run in which no build or test command ran is `not-run`, with a note saying why, never `green`; it is not a hold either, so the CLI still exits 0. `test_suite.py`: both (red before). **Review round (pinned Opus 5, 2026-09-25):** B4-5: a component with no recorded base, or whose tree is gone, was still read as untouched; all three unanswerable shapes now run the component. A run with no worktree record and no `--changed` is `not-run` with `unread`, and P5 holds on it, naming `--changed`. B4-7: the notes name the component and git's reason, never the tree's machine path, because they are committed in `50-suite.json`. B4-9: a coverage command that ran counts as a command that ran. Tests red before. **Review round 2 (pinned Opus 5, 2026-09-25):** B4-10: an in-place (`deny_raw_git`) component was recorded with no base, so after round 1 it ran at P5 on every run. `worktree.plan` now records its base, a read that `deny_raw_git` allows. B4-13: a name the worktree record holds that the profile no longer declares is noted, rather than refusing the whole of P5; a name typed with `--changed` is still refused. B4-11: an untracked file is deliberately not a change. The harness's change is what P4 commits, and counting untracked files would count P5's own reports. Tests red before. **Review round 3 (pinned Opus 5, 2026-09-25):** B4-14: components sharing one checkout each got the whole repository's diff; the diff is scoped to the component's own directory (`--relative -- .`), a no-op for a component that is its repository's root. B4-16: a record naming only components the profile no longer declares holds P5 like an empty record, and says why. Tests red before. Found beside it and filed as T550: worktree mode for components that share a repository. **Review round 4 (pinned Opus 5, 2026-09-25):** B4-17: scoping each diff to its component (B4-14) made a change outside every component's directory, such as a monorepo's root build file, nobody's: nothing ran and nothing was owed. Such a file is now named, and every component sharing that checkout is run as changed, and owed a deploy. The harness's own `.sdlc/` directory is excluded, because a run folder is committed with its change. Test red before, and the control, which fails with the exclusion removed. **Review round 5 (pinned Opus 5, 2026-09-26):** B4-19: a component's directory was claimed by what its diff returned, so components based on different commits (one whose tree was made later) saw each other's history as a change in nobody's directory, and a run that changed nothing owed two deploys. A path is now claimed by any sharing component's directory, asked apart from its diff. B4-18: the exclusion took all of `.sdlc/`, including `.sdlc/deploy/`, which P9's SOP says a deploy reads. Only the harness's own record is excluded now (`suite.HARNESS_STATE`: runs, metrics, graph, corpus, replay, .tmp); the profile and the deploy configuration stay a change. Tests red before; the control fails with the list emptied. The remainder's bound is filed as T552. |
| T503 | P2 | Done 2026-09-25 | In a multi-component workspace, `deploy.py --deploy` without `--component` runs the first component's command from the workspace root and never deploys the others, recording `deployed: true`. (T482 run 3: reproduced by the reviewer, not yet re-run by the lead) Fix: refuse, or deploy each recorded tree. **Fixed 2026-09-25 (batch 4, phase verdicts):** Reproduced by the lead through the failing test. In a workspace of more than one component whose components declare their own `deploy_command`, a deploy that names no `--component` is refused before anything runs. The screen without `--deploy` says so on its own line, because G5 can stand while the deploy is refused. A `--component` that names nothing is refused rather than falling through to the top-level command, and the record names its component. Three tests in `test_deploy.py` (red before). The record half, each named deploy overwriting the one record, is T549. **Review round (pinned Opus 5, 2026-09-25):** The deploy model was redesigned. A deploy is one act where the workspace has one component or no component declares its own `deploy_command`. Otherwise it is by name: each component this run changed deploys with its own command, or the top-level one it inherits (the installer's "blank = inherit"). B4-1: P9's judge read only the first component, so a first component with no deploy excused the rest. B4-2: a named component deploys from its own tree or checkout, never another component's, and an unreadable worktree record is refused rather than read as the root. B4-3: a component inheriting the top-level deploy is deployed by name and owed. B4-4: only the components this run changed are owed, read from P5's own diff against each recorded base; the worktree record alone holds every component by default. B4-8: the P9 prompt says to add `--component NAME`. A missing `--component` exits 2 at the door, not 3. Tests red before. **Review round 2 (pinned Opus 5, 2026-09-25):** B4-10 makes an in-place component diffable, so it is owed a deploy only when this run changed it. B4-11: a run that changed nothing is told so, instead of being told its components declare no deploy_command. Tests red before. **Review round 3 (pinned Opus 5, 2026-09-25):** B4-14: with the diff scoped to each component's directory, components sharing a checkout are owed a deploy only for their own change. Test red before. |

| T511 | P3 | Done 2026-09-25 | `learning --apply` rewrites a CRLF profile as LF, and `--undo` restores the LF text rather than the original bytes. (T482 run 3: reproduced by the reviewer, not yet re-run by the lead) **Fixed 2026-09-25 (batch 2, line endings):** Reproduced by the lead through the failing test. `apply` reads the profile's exact text, renders on an LF view and writes the file's own line endings back; the `before` copy an undo restores is the exact original, and the drift readers compare exact text, so a CRLF profile is not falsely drifted. The existing undo test had hidden it on Windows: its fixture writes CRLF there and both reads fold. `test_learning.py`: bytes compared through apply and undo (red before) and an LF control. **Review round (pinned Opus 5, 2026-09-25):** F2 (P2): digests over exact bytes made `--history` and `--undo` call a profile hand-edited when git had checked it out the other way on another machine; the profile is not under `.sdlc/.gitattributes`, and the audit log is committed. The digests are over the LF view again, which is what the old rows hold; `before` keeps the exact bytes, and undo writes them in the current checkout's convention, which on the machine that applied them is exactly the original. A parametrised test for both directions (red before). **Review round 2:** G3, no change needed - audit rows written by the intermediate commit of this branch (exact-byte digests) would read as drifted, but that commit never reaches main on its own, and rows from main read correctly in all four LF/CRLF combinations. |
| T512 | P2 | Done 2026-09-26 | A workspace install renders each `components[].path` relative to the workspace root, while every consumer (`worktree`, `suite`, `intake`, `adr`) resolves it against the target, so an estate install whose target is not the workspace root cannot start a run: `worktree.py --create` and `intake.py` refuse, "not a directory". (T482 run 4: reproduced by the reviewer, not yet re-run by the lead) Fix: render the path relative to the target (`sdlc-init.py`, the component rendering). **Fixed 2026-09-26 (batch 9), to a design signed off after two pinned Opus 5 design reviews (plans/batch-9-design-note.md):** Reproduced by the lead through a real build. Component paths stay relative to the workspace root, as discovered and as a `[[component]]` author writes them; the profile says where that root is from the target in one top-level field, `workspace_root`, written only when it is not `.`, so an install whose target is the root is unchanged. `policy.component_dir` is the one way a component becomes a directory, at all eleven sites (`worktree`, `suite`, `intake`, `adr`, `deploy`, `review/sca`), guarded by a detector test. A root no relative path reaches (another drive) was refused at exit 1 with nothing written, until T582 replaced the refusal with withholding the run steps; a cadence job whose checkout cannot reach the root is withheld with the reason while the install goes ahead; a re-run over a profile that does not name its root prints the one-line fix, not `--force` (T581). Tests red before, spawned: an install above the root starts a run, one beside its components names `..`, the drive refusal, the withheld job, the re-run NOTE, and `adr.locate` naming a file as the workspace root does. **Reworked after the batch-9 code review:** the re-run NOTE reads the kept line as the consumers' reader does and says "change" for a wrong value (B9-7); the join detector covers `Path(target, ...)`, `.joinpath`, `os.path.join` and either quote, keyed on the `path` subscript, with `learning.py`'s two proposal joins allowlisted by reason (B9-8); the run lifecycle is exercised end to end, spawned, from a target that is not the root - intake, worktree create, list and remove, and the supply-chain gate (B9-9; the withheld-job check stays in-process, through the installer's own predicate); the field is documented at the profile schema's top level (round 2 moved it out of the `release:` block, B9-15, with a test that every top-level key an install writes is described) and by a trailing comment on the rendered line (B9-11); the installer's mirror of the reader is held equal to `policy.workspace_root` over a spelling table (B9-18). The cross-drive refusal's breadth (B9-6) went to the owner, who ruled to withhold rather than refuse: T582. |

A string-valued `permissions.allow` is split into characters, and a non-object `permissions` crashes. (T482 run 4: reproduced by the reviewer, not yet re-run by the lead) Fix: leave the file alone and name it, as `register_mcp` does for `.mcp.json`. **Fixed 2026-09-26 (batch 6, installer):** Reproduced by the lead through the failing test. `settings_op` reads `.claude/settings.json` before merging, as `register_mcp` reads `.mcp.json`. Tests red before. **Review round 3 (pinned Opus 5, 2026-09-26, MERGE with findings):** R2-2: the causes named for a record reading higher than the tree include units moved deeper than the scan reads, and the refusal says the numbers cannot tell them apart. | T517 | P3 | Done 2026-09-26 | An existing generated file that cannot be written (read-only, or locked) crashes the installer with `PermissionError` at exit 1 after other files were already written, although exit 1 is documented as "nothing was installed". (T482 run 4: reproduced by the reviewer, not yet re-run by the lead) **Fixed 2026-09-26 (batch 6, installer):** Reproduced by the lead through the failing test. `install` asks every write it owes before making the first (`cannot_write_reason`: an existing file opened for update and closed, and the nearest existing ancestor of a new one); anything it finds refuses the run at exit 1 with each file named and nothing written, dry run included. A write that still fails (what no look can see: an ACL on Windows, a disk that fills) becomes that file's blocked outcome with a NOTE, the rest land, and nothing is recorded as installed for it. The epilog and README say what exit 1 now covers. Tests red before, spawned; the read-only case runs where a read-only bit binds (not as root). **Review round 1 (pinned Opus 5, 2026-09-26, MERGE with findings):** B6-1: a write that failed after the check ended at exit 0 under "Next: commit what was just installed", having drawn the pipeline from a profile that was not there. It is now exit 4: the scaffold is incomplete, nothing is built on it, and the sign-off is withheld (epilog and README say so). B6-7: the digest half of the test is proven where it can fail, on the preserve-capable profile over a forced re-run. The check opens with write alone, so a write-only file is not refused, and the target directory is named as such. Tests red before; the write-only case runs where POSIX modes bind. **Review round 2 (pinned Opus 5, 2026-09-26, MERGE with findings):** R1-1: a run whose every write fails wrote nothing, so it exits 1 ("nothing was installed"), not 4 ("after others were"). Test red before. **Review round 3 (pinned Opus 5, 2026-09-26, MERGE with findings):** R2-1, a regression from R1-1's fix: a write that fails after its open (a disk that fills) has already created or emptied the file, and counting only completed writes, the run said "nothing was installed" at exit 1 over empty files. What reached the disk is now measured (bytes before and after the failed write; unreadable counts as touched): exit 1 only when nothing did, otherwise exit 4, with a NOTE that the file may be left partly written. Test red before. **Review round 4 (pinned Opus 5, 2026-09-26): MERGE, nothing to fix before merge.** Its two findings, on how a write that fails midway is reported, are filed as T570 and T571. |

| T529 | P2 | Done 2026-09-26 | A `[[canonical]]` claim whose path has a directory part covers every file under that path's PARENT (`extractors/_canonical`, `covers`), so a superseded deployment in a sibling directory is read as canonical: the service resolves to the legacy topic and no `superseded-declaration` row is written. Tests red before: the review's compose reproduction, a nested chart, a directory claim's sibling; the test that pinned the parent rule now pins the package rule. The golden chains' answer keys are unchanged. **Reworked after the batch-8 review:** asked of the nearest chart alone, a claim on an umbrella chart's `values.yaml` no longer reached its subcharts, and a claim naming one of a chart's templates or a values overlay (`values-prod.yaml`) designated nothing, so a superseded tree won in silence (B8-1, B8-2). The helm reader now hands `covers_package` every chart a template ships in (`chart_roots`: its own, then each umbrella that vendors it under `charts/`) and the chart a claimed file belongs to (`claimed_chart`: a template, a values file or `Chart.yaml`); a chart nested anywhere else stays its own. Tests red before, through the reader; a claim naming another file beside the charts (a compose file) still names none. A claim that designates nothing at all is still silent: T573. |

| T531 | P2 | Done 2026-09-26 | Production Java is classified as test code and dropped by every gated extractor, with no unresolved row: `repo_walk`'s test-file rule treats any name ending `it.java` (`Credit.java`, `Audit.java`, `Deposit.java`) and any `it/` package segment as a test. On Windows, `glob_match`'s case-folding `fnmatch` also mints a `test:` node for such a file, so the same commit yields a different graph by platform. (T482 run 6: reproduced by the reviewer, not yet re-run by the lead) Fix: require a word boundary before the suffix, stop treating every `it/` segment as a test directory, and use `fnmatchcase`. **Fixed 2026-09-26 (batch 9), to a design signed off after two pinned Opus 5 design reviews (plans/batch-9-design-note.md):** Reproduced by the lead through a real build. One definition of test code, the same on every platform. `looks_like_test` matches only the suffixes whose boundary is a capital letter (`*Test.java`, `*IT.java`, ...) case-sensitively; `test_*` and the dotted or underscored suffixes keep their folded match, so nothing else narrows. A test file NAME counts anywhere; below a `src/main/` source set a directory name is a package name; `src/test/` and `src/it/` are test source sets; a bare `it` directory is no test directory. `glob_match` is case-sensitive everywhere, and the build's activation walk reports a file NO extractor claims that some extractor's glob matches once case is ignored (`off-case-input`, from `find_files`' `near_misses` in the same walk, carried in the component's shard). The core change is in its own commit with its `core_baseline.md` entry. A regression guard keeps the tests extractor's globs inside `looks_like_test`. Both golden fixtures build byte-identical. Tests red before: the spawned four-controller build, the classifier table, the glob case check under a folding `normcase`, near misses from both walk backends, the off-case row and its shard. T576 stays open (its stated fix differs). **Reworked after the batch-9 code review:** a project kept under a test directory is test code again (B9-1: a fixture's endpoints had entered the graph as production; round 2 took back a first reach that also counted a directory named `it` there, which dropped Italy's site module - B9-14 - so `it` counts only as `src/it/`, and an `it` module's own `src/main/` is the named remainder); the off-case row is one per group of files missing the same globs, names every glob and every extractor declaring one, is part of the component's freshness stamp, and measures against the whole registry under `--only` (B9-2, B9-3, B9-4, B9-13); near misses are worked out only when asked for, in one order on every platform, and the fd half of their test drives the real fd path (B9-10, B9-12). Second and third core commits, each with its own `core_baseline.md` entry. The workspace report reads a lowercase `dockerfile` and `jenkinsfile` (B9-5, B9-16), and its site for a grouped row says how many files it stands for (B9-17). Tests red before. |

(T482 run 6: reproduced by the reviewer, not yet re-run by the lead) Fix: decode with `utf-8-sig`, once, for every reader. **Fixed 2026-09-26 (batch 8, extractors):** Reproduced by the lead through the failing test. `repo_walk.read_text_reporting` decodes `utf-8-sig`: a leading byte-order mark is read past once, for every reader; one anywhere else is content. | T536 | P3 | Done 2026-09-27 | Compose and chart environment values are pushed through YAML 1.1 typing, so `1.10` is recorded as `1.1`, `yes` as `True` and `off` as `False` (`compose.environment_pairs`, `helm`). | T550 | P3 | Open | Components that share one git repository do not work in worktree mode. With the default branch pattern, the second component's `git worktree add` collides on the branch name, so `worktree.create` refuses. With per-component patterns, each tree is a worktree of the WHOLE repository and the component sits at `home/<its directory>`, but P5 runs its commands, and P9 its deploy, at `home`, the repository's root. Components that are their repository's root, and in-place components, are unaffected. Found beside B4-14 (batch 4) and measured on a two-component repository; it predates batch 4. | T551 | P3 | Done 2026-09-30 | In a workspace that deploys as one act, a deploy naming no component runs from the workspace root whenever the run has more than one tree. `deploy._worktree_for` takes the recorded tree only when there is exactly one, so a multi-component one-act workspace deploys the base rather than the run's trees, which is item 37's hazard. This behaviour predates batch 4, and it is arguably right for a workspace-wide command. Found by the batch-4 review. **Owner ruling A (2026-09-28):** a whole-workspace deploy (one act, several components) runs at the workspace root only once the root checkout of every component the run changed holds the run's change - compared by file content, since a squash merge rewrites commits - and otherwise refuses, naming the checkouts that lack it and the two ways on (pull the merged change, or give the components their own `deploy_command`); single-tree runs keep deploying from their tree. Placed in batch 11 (owner, 2026-09-30). **Fixed 2026-09-30 (batch 11):** `deploy.root_lacks` asks, for an unnamed deploy of a run whose worktree record holds more than one component, whether the checkout at the root holds each tree's change: every path committed on the run's branch since its base must read there as it does at the branch's commit. git compares them - content, mode, symlink and submodule pointer alike - never by commit, so a squash merge is held. A component changed in place is held by definition; a tracked change a tree never committed (which no merge can bring to the root), a change nobody can read, and a checkout that cannot be compared with its tree are each named, never assumed held. Until the root holds the change, `run` refuses, naming the checkouts and the two ways on; the CLI exits 2 with it (the caller's to fix, not 3), the preview names it first, and a record nobody can read is could-not-look (3) in the preview as in the deploy. Tree-less, single-tree and by-name deploys are unchanged. Tests red before: the refusal, then the way on after a squash merge accepted; a symlink, a submodule pointer, an executable bit, a file named like a pattern and one whose name begins with a space each refused and then held; an uncommitted change named; a component below its repository's root; an unreadable tree and an uncomparable one named; an in-place component held; the CLI and the preview as spawned processes. **Review round 1 (pinned Opus 5, 2026-09-30):** REWORK - the first version compared the bytes of regular files, so it refused for ever over a symlink or a submodule and passed a change to the executable bit alone (B11-1, B11-3), and its in-place exclusion had no test (B11-2); both fixed as above, and the refusal names the checkout its paths are relative to (B11-5). **Review round 2 (pinned Opus 5, 2026-09-30):** REWORK - a tracked change a tree never committed was held silently (B11-7), and two new behaviours, leaving the run folder out and taking each path literally, had no test (B11-8); both fixed, each test shown to fail without what it defends, and `-z` output is read unstripped so a leading space survives (B11-10). **Review round 3 (pinned Opus 5, 2026-09-30):** REWORK - the executable-bit case's fixture set the bit in git's index but not on disk, which Linux, where git records the bit, read as an uncommitted change, so CI would have failed (B11-12); fixed, with raw output read as bytes so a CR in a path survives (B11-13), the uncommitted change's ways on put cheapest first and fitted to a submodule (B11-14, B11-16), and the workspace checkout's naming tested (B11-15). The tests' squash merges name a committer identity, since a merge that is not a fast-forward needs one and CI's container has none (local CI on the round-3 head, B11-17). **Review round 4 (pinned Opus 5, 2026-09-30):** SIGN-OFF; after it, raw output is decoded losslessly so a path that is not UTF-8 survives (B11-18), and the cheaper way on, discarding an uncommitted change, is followed by a test (B11-19). **Review round 5 (pinned Opus 5, 2026-09-30):** SIGN-OFF of that delta. Carried: the discard test could hold a committed change before its uncommitted edit, so its acceptance compares a path (B11-23), and a path that is not UTF-8 prints as `?` on a UTF-8 console (B11-25). |

Tests red before: a sibling project, a `buildSrc/` input and a directory beside the workspace are named and owe nothing, and the separate-repository reading; a root build file, a middle directory's build file and the workspace's own file still count (controls). |

Test red before: a forced re-run whose write of the profile fails at its first write leaves the profile byte-for-byte as it was, and nothing beside it; the link survives (a control). **Review round 1 (pinned Opus 5, 2026-10-01):** REWORK - the replace met four cases the in-place write had not: on Windows a file another program holds open, even only to read it, refuses the replace (B12-1) - written in place there, by the owner's ruling A of 2026-10-01; a read-only target left a read-only temp beside it, named in the error (B12-2) - refused now before anything is written, by its own name; on POSIX a directory that takes no new entry (B12-4) - named by the pre-check now; and a dangling link pointing outside made directories out there (B12-5) - directories are made only for a path that is not a link. Each has a test, red on the round-1 head. **Review round 2:** SIGN-OFF, with its follow-ups taken, each test red before: the gate before the replace opens the file for writing, as the pre-check already did, instead of asking `os.access`, which on Windows sees only the read-only flag (corrected in round 3, B12-21) - measured 2026-10-01, a deny-write ACL let the replace overwrite the file, where `main` was refused; a file with other hard links is written in place, and the pre-check no longer asks its directory for a new entry (B12-15); whatever stands at the temp name is removed, never followed - a link there had the content written where it pointed (B12-14, red on Linux); and a failed write's error names the target alone, never `-> None` (B12-13). **Review round 3:** SIGN-OFF; two remainders filed - T598 (B12-18) and T599 (B12-19). |
| T571 | P4 | Done 2026-10-01 | A failed write to a file whose bytes cannot be read, on either side of the write, is reported as "the write had begun, so the file may be left partly written". Fix: say the file could not be read either side of the write. Found by the batch-6 review (R3-2). Placed in batch 12 (owner, 2026-09-30). **Fixed 2026-10-01 (batch 12):** a failed write to a file that cannot be read before or after it says so, and that whether it changed is unknown, not that it may be partly written; exit 4 stays. Placed in batch 15 (owner, 2026-09-30). **Batch 15b (owner, 2026-10-04 to 2026-10-07):** RULED A (ruling 12) - one `canonical-unmatched` row per `[[canonical]]` block that designates nothing, citing its line, judged only where its owner was read in a run that saw the whole workspace; the B8-10 shapes with it. RULED A (ruling 14) - among the packages a path designates, the most specific wins, as on `main`; no decision only where even that is ambiguous. RULED A (ruling 16) - a `--from-snapshot` load computes `--check-fresh`'s verdict and counts a stale shard as narrowing the run. RULED A (ruling 17) - the freshness stamp digests only what a reader reads: the `[[canonical]]` blocks, the layout and the profile by name; REFINED A (2026-10-07) - each shard's stamp also holds its own component's path as written, role, stack and shallow flag. RULED A (ruling 20) - a chart directory with a `Chart.yaml` and no values file is a deployment package whose unnamed service is reported, unless it is a library chart or has no templates of its own. RULED A (ruling 21) - every `unread` deployment node is left out of `validate.py`'s orphan metric, the verdict change on some graphs `main` builds intended. Design: `plans/batch-15b-design-note.md` v14, signed off by the owner 2026-10-07 after 13 pinned design rounds, with no 14th (ruling 24: the implementation's red-first tests and pinned code reviews take the remaining edges). **Fixed 2026-10-07 (batch 15b):** `canonical-unmatched`, one row per first block per service, cited at its line, judged only where the owner was read (a deployment reader was handed one of its files, or missed one by case) in a run that saw the whole workspace: (a) the graph read no claim of the owner on the service - saying what it read, never that the owner deploys nothing, naming any deployment file it could not read first and in the spelling its kind needs, and naming what the graph does not read; (b) the path reaches none of the owner's files for the service. `_canonical` holds one reading of a block per reader (`designates`, `names_exactly`, `is_root_path`, dispatched on the facts the readers publish, `kind` and `ships_in`), so `mark-canonical` decides by each reader's rule, the most specific package winning by an ordered test (ruling 14); a chart template, a values overlay and `Chart.yaml` now decide where `main` decided nothing, a root path is recorded `owner=`, a contest row whose block decides nothing says so, and `canonical-duplicate` cites the ignored block. Every deployment file a deployment reader could not read is an `unread` node - handed and unreadable, unparsable or naming no service; a chart with only a `Chart.yaml` and YAML templates of its own (ruling 20); and a file lost to its case, with the new `deployment-off-case` row where T531's does not already name it. A narrowed run - `--components` leaving a component out, `--only`, a failed deployment reader or one with no parser, a load over a missing, refused or stale shard (ruling 16) - judges no accounting row and says so in one line. The freshness stamp digests only what readers read (ruling 17, refined: each shard's stamp also holds its own component's path as written, role, stack and shallow flag). **Upgrade notes:** re-publish snapshots - helm deployment nodes gain `kind` and `ships_in`, host-map files `ships_in`, and unread deployment files their nodes and rows; a shard published before this batch still loads, keeping `main`'s decisions, but its load counts as narrowed until re-published; a snapshot load computes `--check-fresh`'s verdict (seconds) and its report gains `notes`; an `[[alias]]`, `[[entrypoint]]`, `[[apart]]` or `[[generated]]` edit, a pipeline field, a comment line above a block or a move of the tree no longer marks shards stale, a `[[canonical]]` edit still does (and an `[[apart]]` edit does again since batch 24, T621 A'), and one component's re-spelt path or role edit stales that component alone; a root chart file reaches `charts/` only when the root is itself a package; a component holding charts or values files but no templates now runs `helm-configmaps`; `validate.py`'s orphan count leaves `unread` nodes out, so a graph `main` builds can move from FAIL to PASS (ruling 21); the MCP node listing shows `unread`; the OKF export counts unread files on a line of their own (`deploy:unread` in `bundle.json`); `--check-fresh` gains a clause for a deployment reader's case-lost file; `helm-configmaps`' `grammar-unavailable` sentence is its own; extractors may declare `DEPLOYMENT_READER`, `CLAIM_GLOBS` and `GLOB_PURPOSES`. **Review round 1 (2026-10-07): REWORK**, all taken: a `Chart.yaml` is spoken for only by its own chart's values file - read, unread or lost to its case, by one `_canonical.names_values_file` the helm reader shares - never by another file in its directory, which hid a second compose package there and said none of the component's files could be read (B15b-1); a component's root is spelled `.` in the chart and host-map clauses, and a root `path` that ranks the owner's host maps is used (D15b-95), so the clause's way on is accepted when followed (B15b-3); the `--propose-canonical` line counts contested rows, as its draft does, and the draft names the rows it left by their `service` (B15b-4; the two blocks for one name it exposed, on `main` too, are filed as T623); and the belts no test held - a host-map file without `ships_in` (B15b-5), a chart directory the reader cannot list (B15b-7), a reader's two spellings of one claim file (B15b-8) - each get one. **Review round 2 (2026-10-07): REWORK**, taken: the `Chart.yaml` rule is pinned on a second package beside a chart's unread values file (B15b-12); a library chart's values file is no deployment package - helm never installs the chart - so it publishes no node, claim or row and the chart is read, declaring nothing, where its `unnamed-deployment` row's remedy published a claim (B15b-13); where host maps ship in more than one root, the root chart is offered by its own host-map file, since `.` designates every package and ranks none (B15b-14); the chart file names are one fact, `_canonical`'s, the helm reader's globs included (B15b-15); and the OKF README lists every node type in one sorted list (round 2's note). Upgrade note: a library chart's values file that reads no longer publishes a deployment node, a claim or an `unnamed-deployment` row; one that cannot be read or parsed keeps its row and node (round 3). **Review round 3 (2026-10-07): REWORK**, taken: the library rule withholds a claim, never a read - round 2 skipped a library chart's values file outright, dropping `main`'s `unparsable` and `unreadable-file` rows and naming a broken file among those the graph read; it now keeps them and its `unread` node, as decision 6 gives every deployment file the graph could not read (B15b-17). `type: Library` stays an application chart's claim: helm's own check is exact and its loader refuses any other spelling (`isValidChartType` in `pkg/chart/metadata.go`, release-3.16 and `main`; `c.Validate()` in `pkg/chart/loader/load.go`), so the graph mirrors helm, and a chart helm refuses to load is filed as T624 (B15b-18). **Review round 4 (2026-10-07): REWORK**, taken: a shard published while a deployment reader had no parser holds no deployment fact, yet it loaded as current and the load judged both accounting rows over it, telling a developer to remove correct blocks; a load now reads its shards' own `grammar-unavailable` rows by the build's rule (`unparsed_readers`, one helper for both) and is narrowed, its way on being to install the parser and re-publish (B15b-21) - the publish itself is not refused, as on `main`; the unreadable half of the library rule is pinned (B15b-24). A library chart's lost values file is still named as a lost deployment, though the chart deploys nothing whatever it holds - filed as T625 (B15b-23). Upgrade note: a `[[canonical]]` path naming a library umbrella's own values file now decides for its subchart, the one package it still designates, by the ordered test (B15b-25b). **Review round 5 (2026-10-08): REWORK**, taken: `--incremental` reused a shard published while a deployment reader had no parser - no deployment fact - narrowed, its note saying no parser was installed when one was, and `--check-fresh` calling the shard current, so the SOP's `--incremental --publish-snapshot` re-published it unchanged; RULED A (the owner's ruling 26): such a shard is rebuilt, named "its shard was published while `<readers>` had no parser installed" among the plan's reasons, so with the parser installed the component gets its facts and the next publish fixes the shard (B15b-26); and only a deployment reader's lost parser narrows a run, pinned with `openapi-specs` (B15b-28). Each shard is digested once per plan (B15b-39), and a junk line in a tampered rows file is pinned as a rebuild, never a traceback, as is the new reason's plural (B15b-38). **Review round 8 (2026-10-08): SIGN-OFF**; its non-blocking findings are filed, none taken after the sign-off: a damaged shard's traceback as T628 (B15b-40), the tampered rule's reach as T629 (B15b-41 to B15b-43), a missing shard's dependents' reason as T630 (B15b-44), and T627 widened to the parser-less shard's dependents (B15b-45). |

Pins: `tools/graph/tests/test_nested_components.py`, `test_reads_stay_inside_inputs.py`, `test_canonical_packages.py`, `test_compose_resolve_class.py` and `test_name_join.py`, with the reach declaration's refusal beside the other registration refusals in `test_build.py`. **Review round 1 (2026-10-04): SIGN-OFF**, its non-blocking follow-ups taken: the report's `notes` pinned (B15a-3), the layout reader's docstring (B15a-5), dead per-file bookkeeping removed (B15a-7), respelt component paths pinned (B15a-8), the reach refusal driven through registration (B15a-9), and this note's pins and `.mcp.json` clause (B15a-2, B15a-10). **Review round 2 (2026-10-04): SIGN-OFF**; on this row only a test's report read once (B15a-12). **Review round 3 (2026-10-04): SIGN-OFF**; T616 filed for the declared-child rule's two implementations (B15a-14). **Ruling 10 revised for batch 24 by decision 55 (owner, 2026-10-16)** (design pass 24: a declaration's settings key by its own build's placement where its context is a component's own directory). |
| T592 | P3 | Done 2026-10-02 | `repo_walk`'s two traversal backends disagree, on Windows, about a directory junction inside a component: fd does not descend into it, while the pure-Python fallback's `os.walk` does, since a junction is not `os.path.islink` there, and it recurses where the junction points back at the component's own root (`orders/self/self/...`). On POSIX both skip a symlinked directory. So one Windows workspace gives two graphs depending on whether `third_party/bin` holds fd, and CLAUDE.md's "both backends are asserted equal in tests" has a hole there. Found by the T534 design reviews (D534-79, D534-107). Placed in batch 14 (owner, 2026-09-30). **Fixed 2026-10-02 (batch 14):** `_find_with_python` does not enter a directory that is a link - a symlink, or a reparse point tagged as a symlink or a junction (`st_reparse_tag`; a cloud-files placeholder is a reparse point too and a real directory, which fd walks as well) - so the two backends agree. Measured: the junction back to the root recursed 64 `self/` levels. Test red before on Windows (a junction); on POSIX a symlink, which `os.walk` already left alone, and fd compared wherever it is vendored. **Review round 1 (B14-3):** a link to a file and a broken link still diverged; T608 closes that, so the backends agree on a link to a file too; a file that is neither regular nor a link - a FIFO, a socket - is still returned by the fallback alone (T612). The test's fd leg runs only where fd is vendored, and says so. |

| T595 | P3 | Done 2026-10-14 | The JUnit-report freshness rule races the clock: `suite.baselines` and the engine's own runs take `started = time.time()` and discard a report whose `st_mtime` is earlier, but Linux stamps a file's mtime from a coarse clock that can lag `time.time()` by a tick, so a report written after `started` can read as older and be discarded - `tests` is then None ("no JUnit-XML report was found") at random. Measured by the batch-11 review (B11-20) in the CI image: 2000 of 2000 writes carried an mtime earlier than the `time.time()` taken just before them (worst lag 13 ms), and `tools/tests/test_suite.py` alone failed five of six runs on `main` there. Pre-existing; a local CI run can go red for no fault of the branch under test. Fix direction: take the freshness floor from the filesystem (a marker file's own mtime in the working directory), or allow one clock tick of slack. Placed in batch 19 by ruling 35's closing plan (owner, 2026-10-12). **Fixed 2026-10-14 (batch 19):** the floor is the filesystem's, not the clock's: `suite.report_stamps` records each report's mtime, size and file id before the command, and a report counts when the command created or rewrote it - at every door that counts (the component baselines, `intake.baseline`, the engine's steps and both coverage floors). Not the planned clock slack, which lets the step before a command - a build that ran the tests - hand it a report written inside the slack. **Upgrade notes:** a report on disk from before no longer counts for a command that did not rewrite it, whatever its mtime; `tests_reported`, `failing_reported`, `find_report` and `changed_coverage` take `before=` where they took `not_before=`. **Review round 1 (2026-10-14): REWORK** elsewhere; taken here: B19-4 - a reader handed a snapshot reads to the snapshot's depth, whatever depth it is given (`ReportStamps`), so a report the snapshot never looked at is not taken for one the command wrote. **Review round 2 (2026-10-14): REWORK** elsewhere; no finding here. **Review round 3 (2026-10-14): REWORK** elsewhere; no finding here. **Review round 4 (2026-10-14): REWORK** elsewhere; no finding here. **Review round 5 (2026-10-14): REWORK** elsewhere; no finding here. **Review round 6 (2026-10-14): SIGN-OFF**; no finding here. |
| T596 | P3 | Done 2026-10-14 | `suite.changed_files` reads `git diff --name-only` without `-z` and without `core.quotepath=false`, so git quotes any non-ASCII path and P5 records the quoted octal spelling (`"caf\303\251.txt"`) as the changed file - a valid UTF-8 name such as `café.txt`, on Linux and Windows alike. The quoted spelling matches no coverage entry (`match_file` answers nothing, so that file's changed-coverage row is silently absent), and it cannot start with a component's directory, so T552's remainder reads it as lying in no component's directory. Measured on Windows (2026-10-07, plain git in a temp directory): a clone made with `core.autocrlf=true`, refreshed after 2 s, then the attributes added - the printed `git checkout -- <file>` left CRLF in 0 of 3 trials, and `git checkout HEAD -- <file>` in 0 of 3; deleting the file and then running `git checkout -- <file>` restored LF in 3 of 3 and left the tree clean; with the refresh at once (inside the racy window) the printed remedy worked in 3 of 3. | T671 | P4 | Deferred | A chart whose registry the helm reader does not publish keys as Docker Hub, and a proposal states that image as fact: a non-string `image.registry` (`5000`, a mapping, a list, `true`) is dropped with no row of any kind - its quoted twin (`'5000'`) keys `docker.io/5000/org/web` - and a registry under another values key (`global.imageRegistry`, the common chart spelling) is not read at all, so `image-join-proposed` says the chart "runs `docker.io/org/web`" where it renders `myreg/org/web` (batch 23's code review, round 2, B23-9). Not on `main` - task 5 publishes the registry - but the signed-off note's reach ("where the values set `image.registry`"), and nothing moves until a block is declared. Fix direction: a non-string registry read as compose reads a non-string `image:` - a row, and the chart's image unreadable rather than Docker Hub's; a registry in another values key named as the values-convention reader's limit, or read where the design names the keys it may live in. Deferred to after 1.0 by ruling 35. |
| T672 | P4 | Deferred | In a narrowed run, `env-key-unset` and `env-key-from-building-repo` state a cross-component outcome over the components the run read, unhedged - `env-key-unset` says a key is unset once any compose file was read, and `env-key-from-building-repo` names the winner among the components read, where a component the run did not read may set it (design pass 24's review, D24-126). Pre-existing on `main`. Fix direction: in a narrowed run, hedge both as `NARROWED_TAIL` hedges the cross-component rows, or withhold them. Deferred to after 1.0 by ruling 35. |
| T673 | P5 | Deferred | A compose environment value naming a missing variable beside a resolvable one lists the missing variable twice in its `compose-env-unresolved` row ("names A, A"): `props.interpolate` recurses after substituting the resolvable one and concatenates the two lists (batch 24's code review, B24-11). Pre-existing on `main`; batch 24's inherited row reads it too. Fix direction: de-duplicate in `interpolate`, keeping the order. Deferred to after 1.0 by ruling 35. |
| T674 | P5 | Deferred | A chart whose `[[apart]]`-named claim comes from `templates/values.yaml` - a file `helm-deployments` reads as a claim and `helm-configmaps` as a host-map template (D15b-110) - is not withheld from its component's host map: `apart_charts` keys the withheld set by the values file's directory, here `templates/`, which is no chart root (batch 24's code review, B24-13). A chart with two claims, one declared apart, has no single answer. Fix direction: decide whether such a chart is declared apart, and key the set by the chart root (`chart_root`) if it is. Deferred to after 1.0 by ruling 35. |
